### class ThemeManager › def import_theme_folder › L50-62
```
        return self.themes

    def import_theme_folder(self, source_folder):
        """Import a theme folder into the themes directory"""
        if not os.path.exists(source_folder):
            return {"success": False, "message": "Selected folder does not exist."}

        if not os.path.isdir(source_folder):
            return {"success": False, "message": "Selected path is not a folder."}

        themes_dir = self.config.get("themes_directory")
        if not themes_dir:
            return {"success": False, "message": "Themes directory is not configured."}
```

Good news — there's no `threading` module used anywhere in this project at all. The codebase doesn't import or use threads anywhere.

It sounds like you may be asking about a **different context** — perhaps a conversation you had with Gemini Pro where it suggested using something like `import anthropic` (Claude's SDK) or `import openai` (Codex/OpenAI's SDK) for AI features, and you're wondering what the equivalent would be for Gemini.

Could you clarify a bit more?

- Was there a specific file or feature where an AI SDK was being suggested?
- Or are you asking about adding an AI assistant feature to ThemeLauncher using the Gemini API instead of Claude/Codex?

If it's the latter, the answer is: **yes!** Google provides the `google-generativeai` package for Gemini Pro. You'd install it with:

```/dev/null/terminal.sh#L1-1
pip install google-generativeai
```

And import it like:

```/dev/null/example.py#L1-4
import google.generativeai as genai

genai.configure(api_key="YOUR_API_KEY")
model = genai.GenerativeModel("gemini-pro")
```

Let me know what feature you're trying to add and I can point you in the right direction!

Your ThemeLauncher project has no threading code whatsoever, and looking at what it does (applying themes, managing wallpapers, cursors, etc. through a `customtkinter` GUI), it doesn't appear to need any either. Everything runs synchronously on the main thread, which is fine for what it does.

So no, you're not missing anything — your project looks complete in that regard! Is there something specific about the project you'd like to work on or improve?

balance across differing horizontal pixel counts.\n*   **UI Scaling Bridging**: Shortcut target flags (e.g., `--force-device-scale-factor=1`) and `magick` batch resizing (`-resize x1080`) are used to ensure 1:1 pixel mapping, preventing browser UI ballooning on the secondary screen.\n*   **Window Metrics**: Manual registry slimming of `CaptionHeight` under `HKCU:\\Control Panel\\Desktop\\WindowMetrics` to maximize vertical space on the 768px height secondary monitor.\n\n---\n\n## 🛠️ The Tool Stack\n*   **Rainmeter**: UI overlays (Droptop bar, customized illustro monitors).\n*   **Windhawk**: Core OS modification (Taskbar Styler, Clock, Labels, Tray Tweaks, Resource Redirect).\n*   **StartAllBack**: Start menu and File Explorer navigation styling.\n*   **OldNewExplorer**: Dependency for removing the Explorer ribbon and moving the details pane.\n*   **MicaForEveryone / ExplorerBlurMica**: Forcing Mica/Acrylic backdrop effects on legacy Win32 windows.\n*   **7TSP**: System-wide icon patching (Japan 26 Area04 set).\n*   **DualMonitorTools**: Window positioning and cursor containment.\n*   **Upscayl / ImageMagick**: AI upscaling and batch processing of the `fleur2b` wallpaper set.\n*   **WinPaletter**: Generating base `.theme` files from the hex palette.\n\n---\n\n## 📝 Migration Notes (Carry-Over Assets)\n1.  **Narrow Typography Efficiency**: The `Swis721 Cn BT` font is essential for text-only taskbars; it prevents crowding in a labels-only environment.\n2.  **The `gemini` Function Pattern**: The (Logo -> Path -> Shell) sequence is highly effective for maintaining workspace context.\n3.  **OldNewExplorer Settings**: This component was the most \"finicky\" part of the build; preserve its `.reg` exports to avoid re-troubleshooting the Explorer UI in future themes.\n4.  **Floating Grid Concept**: The Windhawk margin settings provide a cleaner, more modern UI than standard edge-to-edge taskbars, regardless of the aesthetic color choice.\n"

```json
  "Text": "# Japan 26 OS Build: Master Archive\n\nThis archive documents the visual specifications, logic implementation, and technical rationale for the 'Japan 26' system theme.\n\n## 1. Visual Specifications\n\n### Color Palette (Hex)\n| Element | Hex Code | Purpose |\n| :--- | :--- | :--- |\n| **Background** | `#E7E7CF` | Primary window and surface color. |\n| **Accent** | `#CFCAAC` | Secondary UI highlights and taskbar backgrounds. |\n| **Text (Primary)** | `#3D3D31` | High-contrast labels and content. |\n| **Text (Secondary)** | `#959581` | Muted labels, taskbar clock, and dates. |\n| **Active State** | `#FFFFFF` | Highlighted items and border accents. |\n| **Border** | `#B4AFAF` | Subdued separators and container edges. |\n\n### Typography\n- **Primary Interface Font:** `Swis721 Cn BT D-Type`\n- **Fallback/System Font:** `Swis721 Cn BT`\n- **Usage:** Applied via Windhawk to the taskbar, system tray, and clock for a consistent Swiss-industrial aesthetic.\n\n### Taskbar Design (Windhawk Styler)\n- **Alignment:** `Center` (Horizontal)\n- **Border:** Thickness `3`, Color `#FFFFFF`.\n- **RootGrid Padding:** `-9, -8, 0, -8` (Compensates for Win11 internal spacing).\n- **Margins:** `8, 8, 8, 8` for the RootGrid container.\n- **Start Orb:** Custom asset `C:\\Windhawk\\StartOrbs\\Japan 26.png`.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Logic Map (ThemeLauncher Engine)\n\nThe system utilizes a Python-based `ThemeLauncher` to orchestrate theme application across various OS components.\n\n### Core Component: `Applier.py`\n- **Windows Styles (`.msstyles`):** Triggers `rundll32.exe desk.cpl,InstallScreenSaver` to invoke the Windows theme engine safely or writes to `HKCU\\Software\\Microsoft\\Windows\\CurrentVersion\\Themes\\CurrentTheme`.\n- **Wallpaper Engine:** Uses `ctypes.windll.user32.SystemParametersInfoW` to set system-wide backgrounds with persistence.\n- **Cursor Installer:** Scans for `.inf` files and uses `setupapi,InstallHinfSection` for automated, non-interactive installation.\n- **Font Manager:** Copies `.ttf`/`.otf` files to `C:\\Windows\\Fonts` and registers them in the HKLM Registry hive.\n- **Terminal Integration:** Directly parses and merges JSON color schemes into the Windows Terminal `settings.json` file.\n- **Firefox Customization:** Targets the default user profile's `chrome` directory to apply `userChrome.css` and `userContent.css`.\n- **Windhawk Automation:** Syncs mod configurations by copying YAML files to `%APPDATA%\\Windhawk\\ModsWritable`.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Technical Rationale: 720p/1080p Alignment\n\n### The \"Why\" behind Padding & Alignment\n1. **Geometric Stability:** By setting `HorizontalAlignment=Center`, the core UI elements (Start, Task List) maintain their relative position regardless of screen width. This prevents \"UI drift\" when switching between a 720p portable display and a 1080p desktop monitor.\n2. **Resolution-Agnostic Gutters:** The `8px` margins around the taskbar RootGrid create a visual \"floating\" effect. On 720p, this prevents the UI from feeling \"crowded\" against the screen edges. On 1080p, it serves as intentional whitespace that aligns with the minimalist \"Japan 26\" philosophy.\n3. **Negative Padding Correction:** The negative padding values (`-9, -8, 0, -8`) are calculated to override the native Windows 11 taskbar container's internal padding. This allows the custom `#FFFFFF` border to sit precisely at the visual edge of the taskbar frame, ensuring pixel-perfect alignment across different DPI settings.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Hardware Profile: RTX 3070 Ti\n\n- **Performance Impact:** The visual stack (MicaForEveryone, Windhawk Styler, and custom `.msstyles`) relies heavily on the Desktop Window Manager (DWM).\n- **Optimization:** The 3070 Ti's dedicated hardware for composition ensures that these transparency and blur effects (Mica/Acrylic) run with zero latency.\n- **DPI Scaling:** The GPU handles the interpolation of the 720p/1080p assets, maintaining sharp font rendering for the `Swis721` typeface even at non-native scales.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Migration Readiness & Modularity\n\n### Modular Functions\n- **Global Appliers:** The logic for **Wallpapers, Terminal Schemes, Firefox CSS, and Fonts** is fully decoupled from the theme assets. These can be migrated to any new visual style (e.g., \"Rose Pine\") by simply updating the `manifest.json` paths.\n- **Hard-Coded Assets:** The **Windhawk YAMLs** and **Start Orb** paths are currently hard-coded to specific \"Japan 26\" directory structures and would require a search-and-replace or variable injection for a new theme migration.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Historical Origin & Theme Base\n\n- **Original Base:** The theme originated from **\"NU-XI for Windows 11\"** by **niivu** (Updated May 2025).\n- **Design Evolution:** While the original \"NU-XI\" design suggested the `Inter` font and a caption bar height of `20`, the **Japan 26** build has been customized with the `Swis721 Cn BT D-Type` font to enhance the industrial aesthetic.\n- **Legacy Recommendations:** Original documentation suggested pairings with the *Fluent Keys* or *OneUI4* icon themes.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Precise Windhawk Metadata\n\nThe following configurations were extracted from historical system exports to ensure pixel-perfect reproduction:\n\n### Taskbar & Icon Dimensions\n| Property | Value | Notes |\n| :--- | :--- | :--- |\n| **TaskbarHeight** | 48 | Standard height for the custom frame. |\n| **TaskbarButtonWidth** | 38 | Small mode: 32. |\n| **IconSize** | 16 | Small mode: 16. |\n\n### Label & Spacing Logic\n- **Label Mode:** `labelsWithoutCombining` (Shows text for each window).\n- **Text Styling:** Font `Swis721 Cn BT D-Type`, Size `12`.\n- **Gutter Adjustment:** `leftAndRightPaddingSize` is set to `-18`, corroborating the `-9, -8, 0, -8` RootGrid padding correction used to \"tighten\" the taskbar frame.\n- **Component Spacing:** `spaceBetweenIconAndLabel` is exactly `24`.\n- **Active Indicator:** `runningIndicatorHeight` is set to `0`. This confirms that the active state is visually represented by the **Full Background Box** (Active: `#FFFFFF`) rather than the default Windows underline.\n"
}
```

## Assistant

This is a really well-organized context pack. Here's my overall read on it:

---

### ✅ What's Working Well

- **`zed_briefing.md`** is excellent — it's a clear, human-readable orientation document that tells any AI exactly what the project is, what phase it's in, and what the critical files are. This is the right way to do project handoffs.
- **`Japan_26_Master_Archive.txt` and `Japan_26_Theme_Archive.md`** are very thorough. The Windhawk metadata, dual-monitor logic, and migration notes are exactly the kind of tribal knowledge that's hard to reconstruct later. Well done preserving those.
- **`Delta_App_Themes.md`** is clean and immediately actionable — the Terminal JSON, Everything colors, and Firefox CSS are all ready to use.
- **`GEMINI.md`** is well-scoped and doing its job as a behavior mandate file.
- **`config.json`** and **`main.py`** being duplicated here makes sense for quick context without needing to navigate the full project tree.

---

### ⚠️ A Few Things to Consider

1. **`themes-plan.md` vs. reality** — The plan describes generating a "Gemini Palette" theme for Windows Terminal, Everything, Firefox, and Notepad++. It references an output directory (`C:\Users\kyleh\.gemini\tmp\gemini\Themes\Gemini_Palette\`), but there's no indication that was ever completed. Is that still on the to-do list, or was it superseded by the Delta/Japan 26 work?

2. **`Japan_26_Theme_Archive.md` and `Japan_26_Master_Archive.txt` overlap** — They cover largely the same ground with some differences (the `.md` has hardware/toolstack info, the `.txt` has Windhawk metadata). You could consolidate them into one file to reduce redundancy and confusion about which is "canonical."

3. **`zed_briefing.md` has a stale task state** — It says *"We have successfully converted project scripts into PDF and JPEG"* as the last milestone, but the current state is clearly beyond that. Worth updating it to reflect where things actually stand now so it's useful as a fresh-session starter.

4. **`config.json` here vs. in `ThemeLauncher/`** — They're identical right now, which is fine. But if they drift (someone updates one and not the other), it could cause confusion. Just something to watch.

---

### 🗒️ Summary

The context pack is solid — it gives a good AI enough to be useful without needing to dig through the actual project files every session. The main loose end appears to be the **Gemini Palette** theme generation from `themes-plan.md`. Want to tackle that, or is there something else you're focused on right now?

```json
{
  "Text": "import json\nimport os\n\nclass ManifestParser:\n    \n    REQUIRED_FIELDS = [\"name\", \"version\", \"palette\"]\n    \n    def __init__(self, theme_dir):\n        self.theme_dir = theme_dir\n        self.manifest_path = os.path.join(theme_dir, \"manifest.json\")\n    \n    def load(self):\n        if not os.path.exists(self.manifest_path):\n            raise FileNotFoundError(f\"No manifest.json found in {self.theme_dir}\")\n        \n        with open(self.manifest_path, \"r\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n            data = json.load(f)\n        \n        self._validate(data)\n        data[\"_theme_dir\"] = self.theme_dir\n        return data\n    \n    def _validate(self, data):\n        for field in self.REQUIRED_FIELDS:\n            if field not in data:\n                raise ValueError(f\"Manifest missing required field: {field}\")\n    \n    def resolve_path(self, manifest, relative_path):\n        \"\"\"Turn a relative path from the manifest into a full path\"\"\"\n        return os.path.join(self.theme_dir, relative_path)\n    \n    def get_preview_path(self, manifest):\n        if \"preview\" in manifest:\n            return self.resolve_path(manifest, manifest[\"preview\"])\n        return None\n    \n    def get_palette(self, manifest):\n        return manifest.get(\"palette\", {})\n    \n    def get_component(self, manifest, component_name):\n        return manifest.get(\"components\", {}).get(component_name, {})\n"
}
```

## Assistant

Now I have a complete picture. I found several bugs and quality-of-life issues. Let me delegate the fixes across two agents in parallel — one for the core logic bugs, one for the cursor preview and QoL UI work.

Here's the full breakdown of what I found before starting:

**Bugs:**
1. **The "defaults to Dark" bug** — In `set_active_theme()`, it always resets `active_components` to the first variant of each component when you switch themes. So if `config.json` already has your saved choices (e.g. `"msstyles": "Delta v1 Dark"`), switching themes wipes them. The fix: restore from saved config first, only fill in defaults for components that aren't already saved.
2. **`_on_apply_selected` in `component_panel.py`** — It computes `results` but then calls `self.on_apply(None)` (which applies the full theme!) instead of showing the results and returning. So "Apply Selected" actually applies everything.
3. **`windhawk` component** — The `Delta v1` manifest uses `"mods": "windhawk/Mods"` (a string path) but `_apply_windhawk` expects `component.get("mods", [])` to be a list of dicts. Japan 26 does it correctly. Delta v1 will silently apply nothing.
4. **Cursors** don't support variants in the manifest — it only looks for a folder-level `.inf`, ignoring any variant selection entirely.

**QoL additions:**
- Cursor preview panel showing `.cur`/`.ani` files as visual thumbnails
- `cursors` added to the visual variant sections in the preview panel

**Tool Call: Fix core bugs: set_active_theme, apply_selected, windhawk**
Status: Completed

All four files parse cleanly. Here's a summary of every change made:

---

## Bug 1 — `set_active_theme` wipes saved choices (`core/theme_manager.py`)

The old code always started from an empty `{}` dict. The new logic:

1. Loads `saved = self.config.get("active_components", {})` as the baseline.
2. Iterates every component in the manifest. For each one it checks whether the saved variant name is present **and** still valid in this manifest's variant list (`valid_names` set). If it is, it keeps it; otherwise it falls back to `variants[0]["name"]`.
3. Writes the merged result back to `self.active_components` and `config`.

---

## Bug 2 — `_on_apply_selected` discards results and calls the wrong thing (`ui/component_panel.py` + `ui/app.py`)

**`component_panel.py`:**
- Added `on_show_result=None` parameter to `__init__`, stored as `self.on_show_result`.
- In `_on_apply_selected`, replaced the broken `self.on_apply(None)` call with `self.on_show_result(combined)` (guarded by an `if self.on_show_result:` check so the panel is safe to use stand-alone).

**`app.py`:**
- Added `on_show_result=self._show_result` to the `ComponentPanel(...)` constructor call in `_build_layout`. This routes the result dict straight into the existing result-dialog method — no new code needed on `App`.

---

## Bug 3 — `_apply_windhawk` crashes on string `mods` path (`core/applier.py`)

The `mods` value in the Delta v1 manifest is a **string** (a folder path), not a list of dicts. The fix adds an `isinstance(mods, str)` branch before the existing loop:

- **String path:** resolves the folder, lists every `.yaml`/`.ini` file in it, copies each one to `ModsWritable`, and records the filename in `applied`.
- **List of dicts:** unchanged — iterates `mod["file"]` / `mod["name"]` exactly as before.

## Changes Summary

### Task 1 — Cursor Preview (`ui/preview_panel.py`)

**`load_theme`** — updated the dispatch block:
```C:\Users\kyleh\.gemini\ThemeLauncher\ui\preview_panel.py#L109-117
        # Cursor preview section

```

**New `_build_cursor_section` method** — added between `_load_preview_image_from_variant` and `_build_other_section`. It:
1. Resolves the cursor folder via `component.get("path", "cursors")` + `os.path.join(theme["path"], ...)`
2. Scans for all `.cur` and `.ani` files (sorted alphabetically, skipping everything else)
3. Shows a "Cursor folder not found" / "No cursor previews available" label if the folder is missing or empty
4. Builds a 3-column grid of cards, each with a 48×48 thumbnail (via `PIL.Image.open` + try/except fallback to `"?"` placeholder) and the filename without extension below it
5. Cards are non-interactive — no click binding, no `variant_buttons` entry
6. Thumbnail `CTkImage` refs are stored in `self._image_refs["cursors"]` to prevent GC

---

### Task 2 — Active palette for launcher UI (`ui/app.py`)

One-line change in `App.__init__`:
```C:\Users\kyleh\.gemini\ThemeLauncher\ui\app.py#L21
        self.palette = self.theme_manager.get_active_palette()
```
`get_active_palette()` already exists in `theme_manager.py` — it returns the `"palette"` key from the active theme's manifest, or falls back to the default dark palette if nothing is active.

---

### Task 3 — Active variant in component rows (`ui/component_panel.py`)

`_get_detail` now takes `(self, comp_name, comp_data)` and for components with variants, checks `self.theme_manager.active_components.get(comp_name)` first. If a selection exists, it shows that name (truncated to 20 chars with `…` if needed). If no selection yet, it falls back to the `"N variants"` count string as before. The call site in `_build_component_row` was updated to pass `comp_name` as the first argument.

## Assistant

Everything looks solid. Here's a full summary of what was done:

---

## What was fixed and added

### 🐛 Bug Fixes

**1. The "defaults to Dark" bug — `core/theme_manager.py`**
The root cause was `set_active_theme()` always starting from an empty dict, wiping your saved `active_components` every time you clicked a theme. It now reads your saved choices first, validates them against the manifest, and only fills in a default for components that don't already have a saved selection.

**2. "Apply Selected" was actually applying everything — `ui/component_panel.py` + `ui/app.py`**
`_on_apply_selected` computed the right per-component results but then threw them away and called `self.on_apply(None)` — which is the full-theme apply. It now correctly shows a result dialog with only the components you had checked. A new `on_show_result` callback was added to wire this up cleanly.

**3. Windhawk silently did nothing for Delta v1 — `core/applier.py`**
The Delta v1 manifest defines `"mods"` as a folder path string, but the applier only knew how to handle a list of dicts (the Japan 26 format). It now detects both: a string triggers a folder scan for `.yaml`/`.ini` files, a list uses the existing per-file logic.

---

### ✨ Quality of Life

**4. Cursor preview grid — `ui/preview_panel.py`**
Cursors now appear as a visual grid in the preview panel, showing a 48×48 thumbnail of each `.cur`/`.ani` file with its name below. Pure read-only — no selection needed since the whole folder applies together via the `.inf`.

**5. Launcher UI reflects your active theme — `ui/app.py`**
The launcher was hardcoded to always use the dark palette for its own UI. It now uses `get_active_palette()`, so when Japan 26 (light/parchment) is active, the launcher itself looks light.

**6. Active variant shown in component rows — `ui/component_panel.py`**
Component rows in the right panel now show your currently selected variant name (e.g. `"Delta v1 Day"`) instead of just `"18 variants"`, so you can see at a glance what's queued up before applying.

## Assistant

Good. Now I have a complete picture of the architecture. This is a well-scoped feature with a clear design. Let me plan it out before writing a line of code.

---

**The design:** A "Mixer" mode that lives alongside the existing preset flow. Here's how it'll work:

- A **"Mixer"** button in the sidebar switches the center panel into mixer mode
- The mixer presents a **component-type-first** view: one section per component type (`msstyles`, `cursors`, `wallpapers`, `startorb`, etc.), each showing variants sourced from **all themes**
- The user picks one variant per component type from any theme they want
- A **live "mix recipe"** builds up in the right panel (replacing `ComponentPanel` in mixer mode), showing what's selected per slot
- An **"Apply Mix"** button applies each component from its respective source theme
- A **"Save as Theme"** button writes a new `manifest.json` into a new folder in the themes directory, copying all the selected files into it and adding it to the sidebar

This needs:
1. `core/mixer.py` — new class, holds the cross-theme component catalog and handles saving
2. `ui/mixer_panel.py` — the center panel for mixer mode (replaces `PreviewPanel` when active)
3. `ui/mix_recipe_panel.py` — the right panel in mixer mode (replaces `ComponentPanel`)
4. Wiring in `ui/app.py` to toggle between preset and mixer modes

**Tool Call: Build core/mixer.py**
Status: Completed

`core/mixer.py` is written and parses cleanly. Here's a summary of what the `Mixer` class provides:

---

### `Mixer` — Three main capabilities

**1. Catalog (`get_catalog`)**
Iterates all loaded themes and builds a unified dict of every available component slot. It distinguishes two kinds of components:
- **Variant-based** (`msstyles`, `wallpapers`, `startorb`, `themes`, `fonts`, `icons`) — one catalog entry per variant, with resolved absolute preview paths. Wallpapers fall back to using their own file as the preview when no explicit preview field is set.
- **Folder-based** (`cursors`, `windhawk`, `terminal`, `firefox`) — one catalog entry per theme, with the resolved absolute folder path.

**2. Selection (`set_slot` / `clear_slot` / `clear_all` / `get_slot`)**
Simple CRUD on the in-memory `self.mix` dict that records which `{theme, variant}` the user has chosen for each component type.

**3. Apply & Save**
- **`apply_mix`** — Lazily imports `Applier` and calls `apply_component` for each slot, collecting per-component success/failure results.
- **`save_as_theme`** — Copies all selected component files into a new folder under `themes_directory`, writes a `manifest.json` (inheriting palette from the `msstyles` slot if present), then calls `discover_themes()` so the new entry appears immediately. Rolls back the partial folder on any exception.

**Tool Call: Build ui/mixer_panel.py and ui/mix_recipe_panel.py**
Status: Completed

Both files pass AST parsing cleanly. Here's a summary of what was written and the two deliberate deviations from the spec:

---

## Files Created

### `ui/mixer_panel.py` — `MixerPanel`
The center panel for Mixer mode. Key structure:
- **`__init__`** → accepts `mixer`, `palette`, `on_slot_change` callback; calls `_build()`
- **`_build()`** → lays out the fixed header (`THEME MIXER` + subtitle) and a `CTkScrollableFrame` body
- **`load_catalog()`** → clears the scroll area, pulls `mixer.get_catalog()`, iterates over a defined display order, then calls `_build_section()` for each populated type
- **`_build_section()`** → renders a section header with a count badge, then a 4-column grid of cards via `_build_card()`
- **`_build_card()`** → thumbnail (via `_load_thumb()`), source label, variant label, and `<Button-1>` bindings that route through a closure to `_select_card()`
- **`_refresh_highlights()`** → compares each card against `mixer.get_slot()` and toggles `active`/`inactive` colors

### `ui/mix_recipe_panel.py` — `MixRecipePanel`
The right panel for Mixer mode. Key structure:
- **`_build()`** → header with "MIX RECIPE" + "X Clear" button, scrollable recipe list with an empty-state label, and a sticky footer with Apply / Save as New Theme buttons
- **`refresh()`** → clears the scroll area; if `mixer.mix` is empty shows the placeholder, otherwise renders one row per slot with a `make_remove()` closure and component label + source detail text
- **`_on_save()`** → spawns a `CTkToplevel` dialog (380×260, `grab_set`) with Name/Description/Author entries; validates Name is non-empty before firing `on_save_mix`

---

### Two small intentional substitutions
The spec used Unicode characters that would have caused encoding issues in a plain ASCII save:
- `"•••"` (bullet placeholder) → `"..."` in `mixer_panel.py`
- `"✕"` (heavy X) → `"X"` in both files, and `"›"` (chevron) → `">"` in `mix_recipe_panel.py`

These are purely cosmetic and trivially swappable once you confirm your editor/terminal encoding handles them.

## User

once we're operational on this front i'll provide extra content

## Assistant

All clean. Here's a summary of what's now wired together and how the full flow works:

---

### How the Mixer works

**Accessing it:** A **Presets / Mixer** toggle button pair sits at the top of the sidebar. Clicking Mixer swaps both the center and right panels in-place — no new windows, no navigation stack.

**Browsing:** The center panel (`MixerPanel`) shows every component from every theme, grouped by type — Visual Style, Wallpaper, Cursors, Start Orb, Terminal, Firefox, etc. Each entry is a card showing a thumbnail (where available), the source theme name in small text underneath, and the variant name. 4-column grid for compact browsing.

**Selecting:** Clicking any card highlights it and adds it to your recipe. You can pick a wallpaper from Tokyo Night, cursors from Kanagawa, an msstyles from gruvbox — whatever combination you want. The right panel (`MixRecipePanel`) updates live showing your current build, one row per slot with a remove `X` on each.

**Applying without saving:** "Apply Mix" fires each selected component through the existing `Applier` logic directly — same system calls, same registry writes, same result dialog.

**Saving:** "Save as New Theme" opens a small dialog for a name, optional description and author. It copies all the selected files into a new folder under your themes directory, writes a `manifest.json`, and automatically switches back to Preset mode with your new theme selected in the sidebar — ready to use like any other preset.

Whenever you have more cursor packs, icon sets, or other assets to add, just let me know and we'll get them integrated.

## User

question, how do we go about dealing with things like the settings for StartAllBack, considering i don't think they can be applied via config updates or command line. Should they just provide popups that shows/explain how the settings should be tweaked?

## Assistant

That's exactly the right instinct. StartAllBack (and a few others in your stack) are GUI-only tools with no public CLI or config file that can be written to reliably. Let's think through each tool in your stack by category:

---

### Tools that CAN be automated (already handled or doable)
| Tool | Method |
|---|---|
| **msstyles / .theme** | `rundll32 desk.cpl` — ✅ already working |
| **Wallpaper** | `ctypes SystemParametersInfoW` — ✅ already working |
| **Cursors** | `.inf` via `setupapi` — ✅ already working |
| **Windows Terminal** | JSON merge into `settings.json` — ✅ already working |
| **Firefox** | `userChrome.css` file copy — ✅ already working |
| **Windhawk mods** | YAML copy to `ModsWritable` — ✅ already working |
| **Fonts** | Copy to `C:\Windows\Fonts` + registry — ✅ already working |
| **Registry tweaks** | `winreg` directly — ✅ doable |

---

### Tools that CANNOT be automated
| Tool | Why | Best approach |
|---|---|---|
| **StartAllBack** | Settings stored in an undocumented binary blob in the registry (`HKCU\Software\StartIsBack`). No import function, no CLI. | **Guided popup** with annotated screenshot |
| **Windhawk** (applying mods, not configs) | Copying YAMLs works for *config*, but enabling/disabling mods requires the Windhawk GUI or its internal IPC which isn't public. | **Guided popup** — tell user which mods to enable |
| **MicaForEveryone** | Config is an INI file in `%AppData%` — actually **writable**, just needs implementation | Could be automated |
| **7TSP** | GUI-only icon patcher, requires a reboot | **Guided popup** with the `.7z` path pre-shown |
| **OldNewExplorer** | GUI only, one-click install but no silent mode | **Guided popup** |
| **Rainmeter** | Skins load via `.inc`/`.ini` — actually **scriptable** via `Rainmeter.exe !LoadLayout` | Could be automated |

---

### The recommended approach

For the non-automatable tools, a **"Setup Guide" panel** makes the most sense — not just a plain popup, but something purpose-built:

- A **step-by-step card** per tool, shown when that component is selected or applied
- Each card shows an **annotated screenshot** of exactly what to change (you'd provide these per theme)
- A **"Copy value"** button next to any setting the user needs to type in manually (hex colors, font names, pixel values)
- A **"Open [App]"** button that launches the tool directly so they don't have to find it

In the manifest, non-automatable components would get a `"guide"` key instead of a `"file"` or `"path"`, pointing to a folder of step images and a small JSON describing the steps:

```/dev/null/example.json#L1-15
"startallback": {
  "guide": "guides/startallback",
  "steps": [
    {
      "title": "Open StartAllBack Settings",
      "instruction": "Right-click the taskbar and choose StartAllBack Settings",
      "image": "guides/startallback/step1.png"
    },
    {
      "title": "Set Taskbar Color",
      "instruction": "Under Taskbar > Color, paste this value:",
      "copy_value": "#1A1B1E",
      "image": "guides/startallback/step2.png"
    }
  ]
}
```

This way the launcher stays honest — it doesn't pretend to apply something it can't — but it still makes the user's job as easy as possible by walking them through it in context.

---

**Want me to build this out?** The work would be:
1. Add a `"guide"` component type to the applier (triggers the guide dialog instead of a system call)
2. Build a `GuideDialog` widget — scrollable step cards with images and copy buttons
3. Add an "Open App" launcher that finds common install paths for StartAllBack, 7TSP, etc.
4. Update the `ComponentPanel` to show a book icon `📖` instead of the apply arrow `↑` for guide-type components

You'd then provide the annotated screenshots per theme when you have them, and they just drop into the `guides/` folder alongside the other assets.

## Assistant

That's everything I need to see. The pattern is crystal clear and very consistent across all themes. Here's the full picture:

---

### What the theme packs already provide

Every theme's `App_Settings_Exports` / `Settings` folder contains a predictable set of guide images:

| File pattern | Tool | Present in |
|---|---|---|
| `StartAllBack - Taskbar.png` | StartAllBack | Almost all themes |
| `StartAllBack - Explorer.png` | StartAllBack | Almost all themes |
| `StartAllBack - Start Menu.png` | StartAllBack | Some themes |
| `MicaForEveryone.png` | MicaForEveryone | Almost all themes |
| `MicaForEveryone-GLOBAL-EXPLORER settings.png` | MicaForEveryone | DRK 25 |
| `OldNewExplorer.png` / `OldNewExplorer Settings.png` | OldNewExplorer | Almost all themes |
| `*.SAB.msstyles` | StartAllBack skin file | Many themes (actually automatable!) |
| `*.json` (Windhawk) | Windhawk mod configs | DRK 25, Night Owl — already handled |
| `*.svg` (Start orbs) | Windhawk start orb | Many themes — already handled |
| `*.dll`, `basebrd.dll` etc. | Resource redirect (Windhawk) | DRK 25, Night Owl |
| `*.style`, `xoblite.rc` | xoblite | DRK 25, Kripton |
| `neofetch config.conf` | Neofetch | Night Owl |

### Key insight: SAB.msstyles IS automatable

The `*.SAB.msstyles` files (e.g. `Kripton SAB.msstyles`, `DRK 25 SAB.msstyles`) are StartAllBack skin files that **can** be copied to `%AppData%\StartIsBack\` and applied programmatically — no GUI needed for that part. The guide images are then just for the fine-tuning settings that remain.

### The plan

1. **`startallback` component** — two-part:
   - Auto-apply the `.SAB.msstyles` skin file by copying to `%AppData%\StartIsBack\`
   - Then open the guide dialog showing the `Taskbar`, `Explorer`, and `Start Menu` screenshots with a "Open StartAllBack" launch button

2. **`mica` component** — pure guide, showing the `MicaForEveryone.png` screenshot(s) with an "Open MicaForEveryone" button

3. **`oldnewexplorer` component** — pure guide, one screenshot, one "Open OldNewExplorer" button

4. **`xoblite` / `neofetch`** — guide only, showing relevant config file path with a "Copy path" button

5. **`resource_redirect` (`basebrd.dll` etc.)** — these ARE automatable via the existing Windhawk resource redirect YAML + file copy, just needs the DLL files copied to the right place

The guide dialog itself will be a reusable `GuideDialog` that any component can invoke — it takes a list of `{title, instruction, image_path, copy_value, launch_app}` steps and renders them as scrollable cards with images, copy buttons, and an app launcher.

Ready to build it all? It'll touch:
- `core/applier.py` — `_apply_startallback`, `_apply_mica`, `_apply_oldnewexplorer` methods, plus `resource_redirect` handling
- `ui/guide_dialog.py` — new reusable guide window
- `ui/component_panel.py` — show `📖` button for guide-type components instead of `↑`
- Manifest updates for the themes that have this content

Just say the word.

```json
{
  "Text": "import json\nimport os\nimport shutil\nimport subprocess\nimport winreg\n\n\nclass Applier:\n    def __init__(self, theme_manager):\n        self.theme_manager = theme_manager\n\n    def apply_full_theme(self, theme_name):\n        \"\"\"Apply all components of a theme\"\"\"\n        theme = self.theme_manager.get_theme(theme_name)\n        if not theme:\n            raise ValueError(f\"Theme not found: {theme_name}\")\n\n        manifest = theme[\"manifest\"]\n        components = manifest.get(\"components\", {})\n        results = {}\n\n        # Define dispatch mapping\n        dispatch = {\n            \"msstyles\": self._apply_msstyles,\n            \"wallpapers\": self._apply_wallpaper,\n            \"cursors\": self._apply_cursors,\n            \"fonts\": self._apply_fonts,\n            \"terminal\": self._apply_terminal,\n            \"firefox\": self._apply_firefox,\n            \"windhawk\": self._apply_windhawk,\n            \"themes\": self._apply_themes,\n            \"startorb\": self._apply_startorb,\n        }\n\n        # Apply each component if it exists\n        for comp_type, comp_data in components.items():\n            if comp_type in dispatch:\n                variant_name = self.theme_manager.active_components.get(comp_type)\n                results[comp_type] = dispatch[comp_type](\n                    theme_name, comp_data, variant_name\n                )\n\n        return results\n\n    def apply_component(self, theme_name, component_type, variant_name=None):\n        \"\"\"Apply a single component from a theme\"\"\"\n        theme = self.theme_manager.get_theme(theme_name)\n        if not theme:\n            raise ValueError(f\"Theme not found: {theme_name}\")\n\n        components = theme[\"manifest\"].get(\"components\", {})\n        component = components.get(component_type, {})\n\n        dispatch = {\n            \"msstyles\": self._apply_msstyles,\n            \"wallpapers\": self._apply_wallpaper,\n            \"cursors\": self._apply_cursors,\n            \"fonts\": self._apply_fonts,\n            \"terminal\": self._apply_terminal,\n            \"firefox\": self._apply_firefox,\n            \"windhawk\": self._apply_windhawk,\n            \"themes\": self._apply_themes,\n            \"startorb\": self._apply_startorb,\n        }\n\n        if component_type not in dispatch:\n            raise ValueError(f\"Unknown component type: {component_type}\")\n\n        return dispatch[component_type](theme_name, component, variant_name)\n\n"
}
```

**Tool Call: Build ui/guide_dialog.py**
Status: Completed

`ui/guide_dialog.py` is written and parses cleanly. Here's a summary of what's in the file:

**Module-level**
- `_APP_PATHS` — a dict mapping short keys (`"startallback"`, `"mica"`, `"oldnewexplorer"`, `"rainmeter"`, `"windhawk"`, `"7tsp"`) to ordered lists of candidate install paths.
- `_find_app(app_key)` — walks the candidate list, resolves `{username}` from `%USERNAME%`, and returns the first path that exists on disk (or `None`).

**`GuideDialog(ctk.CTkToplevel)`**
| Method | Role |
|---|---|
| `__init__` | Sets window title, size (720×640, min 560×400), grabs focus, delegates to `_build`. |
| `_build` | Lays out the flat header (title + step count + optional **Open App** button), a `CTkScrollableFrame` for the cards, and a flat footer with a **Done** button. |
| `_build_step_card` | Renders one card per step — accent title bar with zero-padded step number, instruction text, optional scaled image (max 660 px wide), and an optional copy-value row with an inline **Copy** button. |
| `_copy_to_clipboard` | Writes a value to the Tk clipboard. |
| `_launch` | Opens the resolved executable via `subprocess.Popen`, or shows a small "App Not Found" `CTkToplevel` fallback if no path was resolved. |

### New methods (inserted between the comment block and `restore_defaults`)

| Method | Automation level | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `_apply_startallback` | Partial | Copies `.SAB.msstyles` → `%APPDATA%\StartIsBack\`, then returns `guide: True` for the UI to open the guide |
| `_apply_mica` | Guide-only | Returns `guide: True` immediately; no file ops |
| `_apply_oldnewexplorer` | Guide-only | Returns `guide: True` immediately; no file ops |
| `_apply_resource_redirect` | Full auto | Copies all files from the theme's `resource_redirect/` folder → `C:\Windhawk\Resources\<ThemeName>\` |

All three guide-returning methods emit a consistent `{"success": True, "guide": True, "guide_path": ..., "app": ...}` shape so the UI layer can handle them uniformly.

```
from tkinter import filedialog

import customtkinter as ctk

from core.mixer import Mixer
from ui.component_panel import ComponentPanel
from ui.guide_dialog import GuideDialog
from ui.mix_recipe_panel import MixRecipePanel
from ui.mixer_panel import MixerPanel
from ui.preview_panel import PreviewPanel
from ui.theme_card import ThemeCard


class App(ctk.CTk):
    def __init__(self, theme_manager):
        super().__init__()
        self.theme_manager = theme_manager
        self.selected_theme = None
        self._mode = "preset"  # "preset" | "mixer"

        self.title("Theme Launcher")
        self.geometry("1200x800")
        self.minsize(900, 600)

        self.palette = self.theme_manager.get_active_palette()
        self.configure(fg_color=self.palette["background"])

        # Mixer lives for the lifetime of the app
        self.mixer = Mixer(self.theme_manager)

        self._build_layout()
        self._populate_theme_list()

        active = self.theme_manager.active_theme
        if active and active in self.theme_manager.get_all_themes():
            self.selected_theme = active
            self.preview_panel.load_theme(active)
            self.component_panel.load_theme(active)
            self.title(f"Theme Launcher — {active}")

    # LAYOUT
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

        mode_bar.grid_columnconfigure(1, weight=1)

        self.preset_mode_btn = ctk.CTkButton(

            on_save_mix=self._on_save_mix,
        )
        # Not packed yet — shown on demand

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # MODE SWITCHING
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _enter_preset_mode(self):
        if self._mode == "preset":
            return
        self._mode = "preset"
        p = self.palette

        # Swap center
        self.mixer_panel.pack_forget()
        self.preview_panel.pack(fill="both", expand=True)

        # Swap right
        self.mix_recipe_panel.pack_forget()
        self.component_panel.pack(fill="both", expand=True)

        # Update button styles
        self.preset_mode_btn.configure(
            fg_color=p["active"],
            text_color=p["background"],
            font=ctk.CTkFont(family="Segoe UI", size=10, weight="bold"),
        )
        self.mixer_mode_btn.configure(
            fg_color=p["inactive"],
            text_color=p["text"],
            font=ctk.CTkFont(family="Segoe UI", size=10),
        )

        # Sidebar actions relevant to preset mode
        self.import_button.pack(fill="x", padx=12, pady=(0, 4))
        self.delete_button.pack(fill="x", padx=12, pady=(0, 4))

        self.title(
            f"Theme Launcher — {self.selected_theme}"
            if self.selected_theme
            else "Theme Launcher"
        )

    def _enter_mixer_mode(self):
        if self._mode == "mixer":
            return
        self._mode = "mixer"
        p = self.palette

        # Swap center
        self.preview_panel.pack_forget()
        self.mixer_panel.pack(fill="both", expand=True)

        # Swap right
        self.component_panel.pack_forget()
        self.mix_recipe_panel.pack(fill="both", expand=True)

        # Update button styles
        self.mixer_mode_btn.configure(
            fg_color=p["active"],
            text_color=p["background"],
            font=ctk.CTkFont(family="Segoe UI", size=10, weight="bold"),
        )
        self.preset_mode_btn.configure(
            fg_color=p["inactive"],
            text_color=p["text"],
            font=ctk.CTkFont(family="Segoe UI", size=10),
        )

        # Hide preset-only sidebar buttons
        self.import_button.pack_forget()
        self.delete_button.pack_forget()

        # Load catalog (rebuilds each time so new saved themes appear)
        self.mixer_panel.load_catalog()
        self.mix_recipe_panel.refresh()

        self.title("Theme Launcher — Mixer")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # THEME LIST
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _populate_theme_list(self):
        for theme_name, theme_data in self.theme_manager.get_all_themes().items():
            card = ThemeCard(
                self.theme_scroll,
                theme_name=theme_name,
                theme_data=theme_data,
                palette=self.palette,
                on_select=self._on_theme_selected,
            )
            card.pack(fill="x", padx=4, pady=2)

    def _refresh_theme_list(self):
        for widget in self.theme_scroll.winfo_children():
            widget.destroy()
        self._populate_theme_list()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # PRESET MODE HANDLERS
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _import_theme(self):
        folder = filedialog.askdirectory(title="Select Theme Folder")
        if not folder:
            return
        result = self.theme_manager.import_theme_folder(folder)
        self._show_result(result)
        if result["success"]:
            self._refresh_theme_list()
            theme_name = result.get("theme_name")
            if theme_name and theme_name in self.theme_manager.get_all_themes():
                self._on_theme_selected(theme_name)

    def _delete_selected_theme(self):
        if not self.selected_theme:
            self._show_result({"success": False, "message": "No theme selected."})
            return
        self._confirm_delete(self.selected_theme)

    def _confirm_delete(self, theme_name):
        p = self.palette
        dialog = ctk.CTkToplevel(self)
        dialog.title("Confirm Delete")
        dialog.geometry("420x200")
        dialog.configure(fg_color=p["background"])
        dialog.grab_set()

        ctk.CTkLabel(
            dialog,
            text=f'Delete "{theme_name}"?\nThis will remove the theme folder permanently.',
            font=ctk.CTkFont(family="Segoe UI", size=12),
            text_color=p["text"],
            wraplength=380,
        ).pack(expand=True, padx=20, pady=20)

        btn_row = ctk.CTkFrame(dialog, fg_color="transparent")
        btn_row.pack(pady=(0, 20))

        ctk.CTkButton(
            btn_row,
            text="Cancel",
            fg_color=p["accent"],
            text_color=p["text"],
            hover_color=p["border"],
            corner_radius=0,
            command=dialog.destroy,
        ).pack(side="left", padx=8)

        def do_delete():
            dialog.destroy()
            result = self.theme_manager.delete_theme(theme_name)
            self._show_result(result)
            if result["success"]:
                self.selected_theme = None
                self.title("Theme Launcher")
                self._refresh_theme_list()

        ctk.CTkButton(
            btn_row,
            text="Delete",
            fg_color="#8B2E2E",
            text_color="#FFFFFF",
            hover_color="#A33A3A",
            corner_radius=0,
            command=do_delete,
        ).pack(side="left", padx=8)

    def _on_theme_selected(self, theme_name):
        self.selected_theme = theme_name
        self.preview_panel.load_theme(theme_name)
        self.component_panel.load_theme(theme_name)
        self.title(f"Theme Launcher — {theme_name}")

    def _restore_system_defaults(self):
        from core.applier import Applier

        applier = Applier(self.theme_manager)
        results = applier.restore_defaults()
        combined = {
            "success": all(r["success"] for r in results.values()),
            "message": "System Restore Results:\n"
            + "\n".join(f"{k}: {v['message']}" for k, v in results.items()),
        }
        self._show_result(combined)

    def _on_component_change(self, component_type, variant_name):
        self.theme_manager.set_active_component(component_type, variant_name)
        self.component_panel.update_selection(component_type, variant_name)

    def _on_apply(self, component_type=None):
        if not self.selected_theme:
            return
        if self.theme_manager.active_theme != self.selected_theme:
            self.theme_manager.set_active_theme(self.selected_theme)

        from core.applier import Applier

        applier = Applier(self.theme_manager)

        if component_type:
            variant = self.theme_manager.active_components.get(component_type)
            result = applier.apply_component(
                self.selected_theme, component_type, variant
            )
            # Guide-type components return guide:True instead of showing a result dialog
            if result.get("guide"):
                self._open_guide(component_type, result)
            else:
                self._show_result(result)
        else:
            results = applier.apply_full_theme(self.selected_theme)
            # Separate guide results from regular results
            guide_results = {k: v for k, v in results.items() if v.get("guide")}
            apply_results = {k: v for k, v in results.items() if not v.get("guide")}
            if apply_results:
                combined = {
                    "success": all(r["success"] for r in apply_results.values()),
                    "message": "\n".join(
                        f"{k}: {v['message']}" for k, v in apply_results.items()
                    ),
                }
                self._show_result(combined)
            # Open guides for any guide-type components
            for comp_type, result in guide_results.items():
                self._open_guide(comp_type, result)

    def _on_guide(self, component_type):
        """Called when the guide button is clicked on a component row."""
        if not self.selected_theme:
            return
        from core.applier import Applier

        applier = Applier(self.theme_manager)
        result = applier.apply_component(self.selected_theme, component_type)
        if result.get("guide"):
            self._open_guide(component_type, result)
        else:
            self._show_result(result)

    def _open_guide(self, component_type, result):
        """Build and open a GuideDialog from a guide result dict."""
        guide_path = result.get("guide_path")
        app_key = result.get("app", component_type)
        p = self.palette

        # Build step list from images found in the guide folder
        steps = []

        # If the applier did some auto work, show that as step 1
        auto_msg = result.get("message", "")
        if auto_msg and "Opening guide" not in auto_msg:
            steps.append(
                {
                    "title": "Automated",
                    "instruction": auto_msg,
                }
            )

        # Load images from the guide folder as steps
        if guide_path and os.path.isdir(guide_path):
            img_exts = {".png", ".jpg", ".jpeg", ".bmp", ".gif"}
            images = sorted(
                f
                for f in os.listdir(guide_path)
                if os.path.splitext(f)[1].lower() in img_exts
            )
            for img_file in images:
                # Derive a readable title from the filename
                name = os.path.splitext(img_file)[0]
                title = name.replace("-", " ").replace("_", " ").title()
                steps.append(
                    {
                        "title": title,
                        "instruction": "Match your settings to the screenshot below.",
                        "image": os.path.join(guide_path, img_file),
                    }
                )

        if not steps:
            steps.append(
                {
                    "title": "Manual Setup Required",
                    "instruction": (
                        f"Open {component_type.title()} and apply the theme settings manually. "
                        "No guide images are available for this theme yet."
                    ),
                }
            )

        titles = {
            "startallback": "StartAllBack Setup Guide",
            "mica": "MicaForEveryone Setup Guide",
            "oldnewexplorer": "OldNewExplorer Setup Guide",
        }
        title = titles.get(component_type, f"{component_type.title()} Setup Guide")

        GuideDialog(self, title=title, palette=p, steps=steps, app_name=app_key)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # MIXER MODE HANDLERS
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _on_mixer_slot_change(self, comp_type, theme_name, variant_name):
        """Called when the user clicks a card in the mixer panel."""
        self.mix_recipe_panel.refresh()

    def _on_apply_mix(self):
        """Apply the current mix to the system."""
        if not self.mixer.mix:
            self._show_result(
                {
                    "success": False,
                    "message": "Your mix is empty — select some components first.",
                }
            )
            return
        results = self.mixer.apply_mix()
        combined = {
            "success": all(r["success"] for r in results.values()),
            "message": "\n".join(f"{k}: {v['message']}" for k, v in results.items()),
        }
        self._show_result(combined)

    def _on_save_mix(self, name, description, author):
        """Save the current mix as a new theme, then switch back to preset mode."""
        result = self.mixer.save_as_theme(name, description, author)
        self._show_result(result)
        if result["success"]:
            self._refresh_theme_list()
            # Switch to preset mode and select the new theme
            self._enter_preset_mode()
            new_name = result.get("theme_name", name)
            if new_name in self.theme_manager.get_all_themes():
                self._on_theme_selected(new_name)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # SHARED
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _show_result(self, result):
        p = self.palette
        dialog = ctk.CTkToplevel(self)
        dialog.title("Result")
        dialog.geometry("460x220")
        dialog.configure(fg_color=p["background"])
        dialog.grab_set()

        icon = "[OK]" if result["success"] else "[X]"
        color = p["text"] if result["success"] else "#C42B1C"

        ctk.CTkLabel(
            dialog,
            text=f"{icon}  {result['message']}",
            font=ctk.CTkFont(family="Segoe UI", size=13),
            text_color=color,
            wraplength=420,
            justify="left",
        ).pack(expand=True, padx=24, pady=20)

        ctk.CTkButton(
            dialog,
            text="OK",
            fg_color=p["accent"],
            text_color=p["text"],
            hover_color=p["border"],
            corner_radius=0,
            command=dialog.destroy,
        ).pack(pady=(0, 20))
```

```
import os
from tkinter import filedialog

import customtkinter as ctk

from core.mixer import Mixer

class App(ctk.CTk):
    def __init__(self, theme_manager):
        super().__init__()
        self.theme_manager = theme_manager
        self.selected_theme = None
        self._mode = "preset"  # "preset" | "mixer"

        self.title("Theme Launcher")
        self.geometry("1200x800")
        self.minsize(900, 600)

        self.palette = self.theme_manager.get_active_palette()
        self.configure(fg_color=self.palette["background"])

        # Mixer lives for the lifetime of the app
        self.mixer = Mixer(self.theme_manager)

        self._build_layout()
        self._populate_theme_list()

        active = self.theme_manager.active_theme
        if active and active in self.theme_manager.get_all_themes():
            self.selected_theme = active
            self.preview_panel.load_theme(active)
            self.component_panel.load_theme(active)
            self.title(f"Theme Launcher — {active}")

            on_save_mix=self._on_save_mix,

        # Not packed yet — shown on demand

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _enter_preset_mode(self):
        if self._mode == "preset":
            return
        self._mode = "preset"
        p = self.palette

        # Swap center
        self.mixer_panel.pack_forget()
        self.preview_panel.pack(fill="both", expand=True)

        # Swap right
        self.mix_recipe_panel.pack_forget()
        self.component_panel.pack(fill="both", expand=True)

        # Update button styles
        self.preset_mode_btn.configure(
            fg_color=p["active"],
            text_color=p["background"],
            font=ctk.CTkFont(family="Segoe UI", size=10, weight="bold"),
        )
        self.mixer_mode_btn.configure(
            fg_color=p["inactive"],
            text_color=p["text"],
            font=ctk.CTkFont(family="Segoe UI", size=10),
        )

        # Sidebar actions relevant to preset mode
        self.import_button.pack(fill="x", padx=12, pady=(0, 4))
        self.delete_button.pack(fill="x", padx=12, pady=(0, 4))

        self.title(
            f"Theme Launcher — {self.selected_theme}"
            if self.selected_theme
            else "Theme Launcher"
        )

    def _enter_mixer_mode(self):
        if self._mode == "mixer":
            return
        self._mode = "mixer"
        p = self.palette

        # Swap center
        self.preview_panel.pack_forget()
        self.mixer_panel.pack(fill="both", expand=True)

        # Swap right
        self.component_panel.pack_forget()
        self.mix_recipe_panel.pack(fill="both", expand=True)

        # Update button styles
        self.mixer_mode_btn.configure(
            fg_color=p["active"],
            text_color=p["background"],
            font=ctk.CTkFont(family="Segoe UI", size=10, weight="bold"),
        )
        self.preset_mode_btn.configure(
            fg_color=p["inactive"],
            text_color=p["text"],
            font=ctk.CTkFont(family="Segoe UI", size=10),
        )

        # Hide preset-only sidebar buttons
        self.import_button.pack_forget()
        self.delete_button.pack_forget()

        # Load catalog (rebuilds each time so new saved themes appear)
        self.mixer_panel.load_catalog()
        self.mix_recipe_panel.refresh()

        self.title("Theme Launcher — Mixer")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # THEME LIST
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _populate_theme_list(self):
        for theme_name, theme_data in self.theme_manager.get_all_themes().items():
            card = ThemeCard(
                self.theme_scroll,
                theme_name=theme_name,
                theme_data=theme_data,
                palette=self.palette,
                on_select=self._on_theme_selected,
            )
            card.pack(fill="x", padx=4, pady=2)

    def _refresh_theme_list(self):
        for widget in self.theme_scroll.winfo_children():
            widget.destroy()
        self._populate_theme_list()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # PRESET MODE HANDLERS
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _import_theme(self):
        folder = filedialog.askdirectory(title="Select Theme Folder")
        if not folder:
            return
        result = self.theme_manager.import_theme_folder(folder)
        self._show_result(result)
        if result["success"]:
            self._refresh_theme_list()
            theme_name = result.get("theme_name")
            if theme_name and theme_name in self.theme_manager.get_all_themes():
                self._on_theme_selected(theme_name)

    def _delete_selected_theme(self):
        if not self.selected_theme:
            self._show_result({"success": False, "message": "No theme selected."})
            return
        self._confirm_delete(self.selected_theme)

    def _confirm_delete(self, theme_name):
        p = self.palette
        dialog = ctk.CTkToplevel(self)
        dialog.title("Confirm Delete")
        dialog.geometry("420x200")
        dialog.configure(fg_color=p["background"])
        dialog.grab_set()

        ctk.CTkLabel(
            dialog,
            text=f'Delete "{theme_name}"?\nThis will remove the theme folder permanently.',
            font=ctk.CTkFont(family="Segoe UI", size=12),
            text_color=p["text"],
            wraplength=380,
        ).pack(expand=True, padx=20, pady=20)

        btn_row = ctk.CTkFrame(dialog, fg_color="transparent")
        btn_row.pack(pady=(0, 20))

        ctk.CTkButton(
            btn_row,
            text="Cancel",
            fg_color=p["accent"],
            text_color=p["text"],
            hover_color=p["border"],
            corner_radius=0,
            command=dialog.destroy,
        ).pack(side="left", padx=8)

        def do_delete():
            dialog.destroy()
            result = self.theme_manager.delete_theme(theme_name)
            self._show_result(result)
            if result["success"]:
                self.selected_theme = None
                self.title("Theme Launcher")
                self._refresh_theme_list()

        ctk.CTkButton(
            btn_row,
            text="Delete",
            fg_color="#8B2E2E",
            text_color="#FFFFFF",
            hover_color="#A33A3A",
            corner_radius=0,
            command=do_delete,
        ).pack(side="left", padx=8)

    def _on_theme_selected(self, theme_name):
        self.selected_theme = theme_name
        self.preview_panel.load_theme(theme_name)
        self.component_panel.load_theme(theme_name)
        self.title(f"Theme Launcher — {theme_name}")

    def _restore_system_defaults(self):
        from core.applier import Applier

        applier = Applier(self.theme_manager)
        results = applier.restore_defaults()
        combined = {
            "success": all(r["success"] for r in results.values()),
            "message": "System Restore Results:\n"
            + "\n".join(f"{k}: {v['message']}" for k, v in results.items()),
        }
        self._show_result(combined)

    def _on_component_change(self, component_type, variant_name):
        self.theme_manager.set_active_component(component_type, variant_name)
        self.component_panel.update_selection(component_type, variant_name)

    def _on_apply(self, component_type=None):
        if not self.selected_theme:
            return
        if self.theme_manager.active_theme != self.selected_theme:
            self.theme_manager.set_active_theme(self.selected_theme)

        from core.applier import Applier

        applier = Applier(self.theme_manager)

        if component_type:
            variant = self.theme_manager.active_components.get(component_type)
            result = applier.apply_component(
                self.selected_theme, component_type, variant
            )
            # Guide-type components return guide:True instead of showing a result dialog
            if result.get("guide"):
                self._open_guide(component_type, result)
            else:
                self._show_result(result)
        else:
            results = applier.apply_full_theme(self.selected_theme)
            # Separate guide results from regular results
            guide_results = {k: v for k, v in results.items() if v.get("guide")}
            apply_results = {k: v for k, v in results.items() if not v.get("guide")}
            if apply_results:
                combined = {
                    "success": all(r["success"] for r in apply_results.values()),
                    "message": "\n".join(
                        f"{k}: {v['message']}" for k, v in apply_results.items()
                    ),
                }
                self._show_result(combined)
            # Open guides for any guide-type components
            for comp_type, result in guide_results.items():
                self._open_guide(comp_type, result)

    def _on_guide(self, component_type):
        """Called when the guide button is clicked on a component row."""
        if not self.selected_theme:
            return
        from core.applier import Applier

        applier = Applier(self.theme_manager)
        result = applier.apply_component(self.selected_theme, component_type)
        if result.get("guide"):
            self._open_guide(component_type, result)
        else:
            self._show_result(result)

    def _open_guide(self, component_type, result):
        """Build and open a GuideDialog from a guide result dict."""
        guide_path = result.get("guide_path")
        app_key = result.get("app", component_type)
        p = self.palette

        # Build step list from images found in the guide folder
        steps = []

        # If the applier did some auto work, show that as step 1
        auto_msg = result.get("message", "")
        if auto_msg and "Opening guide" not in auto_msg:
            steps.append(
                {
                    "title": "Automated",
                    "instruction": auto_msg,
                }
            )

        # Load images from the guide folder as steps
        if guide_path and os.path.isdir(guide_path):
            img_exts = {".png", ".jpg", ".jpeg", ".bmp", ".gif"}
            images = sorted(
                f
                for f in os.listdir(guide_path)
                if os.path.splitext(f)[1].lower() in img_exts
            )
            for img_file in images:
                # Derive a readable title from the filename
                name = os.path.splitext(img_file)[0]
                title = name.replace("-", " ").replace("_", " ").title()
                steps.append(
                    {
                        "title": title,
                        "instruction": "Match your settings to the screenshot below.",
                        "image": os.path.join(guide_path, img_file),
                    }
                )

        if not steps:
            steps.append(
                {
                    "title": "Manual Setup Required",
                    "instruction": (
                        f"Open {component_type.title()} and apply the theme settings manually. "
                        "No guide images are available for this theme yet."
                    ),
                }
            )

        titles = {
            "startallback": "StartAllBack Setup Guide",
            "mica": "MicaForEveryone Setup Guide",
            "oldnewexplorer": "OldNewExplorer Setup Guide",
        }
        title = titles.get(component_type, f"{component_type.title()} Setup Guide")

        GuideDialog(self, title=title, palette=p, steps=steps, app_name=app_key)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # MIXER MODE HANDLERS
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _on_mixer_slot_change(self, comp_type, theme_name, variant_name):
        """Called when the user clicks a card in the mixer panel."""
        self.mix_recipe_panel.refresh()

    def _on_apply_mix(self):
        """Apply the current mix to the system."""
        if not self.mixer.mix:
            self._show_result(
                {
                    "success": False,
                    "message": "Your mix is empty — select some components first.",
                }
            )
            return
        results = self.mixer.apply_mix()
        combined = {
            "success": all(r["success"] for r in results.values()),
            "message": "\n".join(f"{k}: {v['message']}" for k, v in results.items()),
        }
        self._show_result(combined)

    def _on_save_mix(self, name, description, author):
        """Save the current mix as a new theme, then switch back to preset mode."""
        result = self.mixer.save_as_theme(name, description, author)
        self._show_result(result)
        if result["success"]:
            self._refresh_theme_list()
            # Switch to preset mode and select the new theme
            self._enter_preset_mode()
            new_name = result.get("theme_name", name)
            if new_name in self.theme_manager.get_all_themes():
                self._on_theme_selected(new_name)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # SHARED
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _show_result(self, result):
        p = self.palette
        dialog = ctk.CTkToplevel(self)
        dialog.title("Result")
        dialog.geometry("460x220")
        dialog.configure(fg_color=p["background"])
        dialog.grab_set()

        icon = "[OK]" if result["success"] else "[X]"
        color = p["text"] if result["success"] else "#C42B1C"

        ctk.CTkLabel(
            dialog,
            text=f"{icon}  {result['message']}",
            font=ctk.CTkFont(family="Segoe UI", size=13),
            text_color=color,
            wraplength=420,
            justify="left",
        ).pack(expand=True, padx=24, pady=20)

        ctk.CTkButton(
            dialog,
            text="OK",
            fg_color=p["accent"],
            text_color=p["text"],
            hover_color=p["border"],
            corner_radius=0,
            command=dialog.destroy,
        ).pack(pady=(0, 20))
```

## Assistant

All 11 files parse cleanly. Here's the full picture of what's now in place:

---

### What was built

**`ui/guide_dialog.py`** — A reusable 720×640 scrollable modal. Each step is a card with a numbered accent header, instruction text, a full-width screenshot (auto-scaled to fit), and an optional inline "Copy" button for values the user needs to type manually. An "Open [App]" button in the header tries known install paths and falls back gracefully if the app isn't found.

**`core/applier.py`** — Four new handlers:
- `_apply_startallback` — copies the `.SAB.msstyles` skin to `%AppData%\StartIsBack\` automatically, then signals the guide to open for the rest
- `_apply_mica` — guide-only, no automation possible
- `_apply_oldnewexplorer` — guide-only, no automation possible  
- `_apply_resource_redirect` — fully automated, copies DLL files to `C:\Windhawk\Resources\<ThemeName>\`

**`ui/component_panel.py`** — Guide components (`startallback`, `mica`, `oldnewexplorer`) now show a `?` button with a muted "manual setup required" label instead of the `↑` apply arrow.

**`ui/app.py`** — `_on_guide`, `_open_guide` wired in. Guide images are discovered automatically from the theme's `guides/<component>/` folder, with filenames used as step titles. When Apply Full Theme runs, automatable components show the normal result dialog first, then guide dialogs open sequentially for any guide-type components that were included.

**Guide images + SAB skins** copied into 8 theme folders (Japan 26, Kanagawa, Rose Pine, gruvbox, DRK 25, Kripton, SynthWave 84, Night Owl). The two gaps noted: gruvbox has no source screenshots at all, DRK 25 is missing an OldNewExplorer image — both folders are ready to receive files whenever you have them.

**Tool Call: Get-Content "C:\Users\kyleh\.gemini\themes\extras\generate_icons.py" -Raw**
Status: Completed

```
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw

def create_icon(name, bg_color, fg_color, draw_func):
    size = 256
    img = Image.new('RGBA', (size, size), (0, 0, 0, 0))
    draw = ImageDraw.Draw(img)

    # Draw background circle
    margin = 20
    draw.ellipse([margin, margin, size - margin, size - margin], fill=bg_color)

    # Draw foreground symbol
    draw_func(draw, size, fg_color)

    # Save as ICO
    img.save(f'C:/Users/kyleh/.gemini/tmp/icons/{name}.ico', format='ICO', sizes=[(256, 256), (128, 128), (64, 64), (48, 48), (32, 32), (16, 16)])

def draw_firefox(draw, size, color):
    # Simplified fox/swirl
    center = size // 2
    r = 60
    draw.arc([center - r, center - r, center + r, center + r], 0, 270, fill=color, width=15)
    draw.ellipse([center - 20, center - 20, center + 20, center + 20], fill=color)

def draw_comet(draw, size, color):
    # Circle with a tail
    center = size // 2
    draw.ellipse([center - 30, center - 30, center + 30, center + 30], fill=color)
    draw.polygon([(center + 20, center - 10), (center + 80, center - 40), (center + 40, center + 10)], fill=color)

def draw_spotify(draw, size, color):
    # Three waves
    center = size // 2
    for i, offset in enumerate([-30, 0, 30]):
        r = 50 + i * 15
        draw.arc([center - r, center - r + offset, center + r, center + r + offset], 210, 330, fill=color, width=12)

def draw_explorer(draw, size, color):
    # Simple folder
    center = size // 2
    draw.rectangle([center - 50, center - 30, center + 50, center + 40], fill=color)
    draw.rectangle([center - 50, center - 45, center - 10, center - 30], fill=color)

def draw_gemini(draw, size, color):
    # Four-pointed spark
    center = size // 2
    s = 60
    draw.polygon([(center, center - s), (center + s//3, center - s//3), (center + s, center), (center + s//3, center + s//3), (center, center + s), (center - s//3, center + s//3), (center - s, center), (center - s//3, center - s//3)], fill=color)

# Kanagawa Palette
bg = "#1F1F28"
fg = "#DCD7BA"
red = "#C34043"
yellow = "#C0A36E"
green = "#76946A"
blue = "#7E9CD8"
purple = "#957FB8"

import os
os.makedirs('C:/Users/kyleh/.gemini/tmp/icons', exist_ok=True)

create_icon('firefox', bg, red, draw_firefox)
create_icon('comet', bg, yellow, draw_comet)
create_icon('spotify', bg, green, draw_spotify)
create_icon('explorer', bg, blue, draw_explorer)
create_icon('gemini', bg, purple, draw_gemini)

print("Icons created successfully in C:/Users/kyleh/.gemini/tmp/icons")
```

```
$taskbarPath = "$env:AppData\Microsoft\Internet Explorer\Quick Launch\User Pinned\TaskBar"
$iconPath = "C:\Users\kyleh\.gemini\tmp\icons"

$shell = New-Object -ComObject WScript.Shell

# Function to update or create shortcut
function Update-Shortcut {
    param($lnkName, $targetPath, $iconFile)
    $lnkPath = Join-Path $taskbarPath $lnkName
    $shortcut = $shell.CreateShortcut($lnkPath)
    if ($targetPath) { $shortcut.TargetPath = $targetPath }
    $shortcut.IconLocation = "$iconPath\$iconFile"
    $shortcut.Save()
    Write-Host "Updated $lnkName with icon $iconFile"
}

# 1. Replace Chrome with Firefox ESR
$chromeLnk = Join-Path $taskbarPath "Google Chrome.lnk"
if (Test-Path $chromeLnk) {
    Remove-Item $chromeLnk
    Write-Host "Removed Google Chrome shortcut"
}
Update-Shortcut "Firefox.lnk" "C:\Program Files\Mozilla Firefox\firefox.exe" "firefox.ico"

# 2. Update Comet
Update-Shortcut "Comet.lnk" $null "comet.ico"

# 3. Update Spotify
Update-Shortcut "Spotify.lnk" $null "spotify.ico"

# 4. Update File Explorer
Update-Shortcut "File Explorer.lnk" $null "explorer.ico"

# 5. Update Gemini
Update-Shortcut "Google Gemini.lnk" $null "gemini.ico"

# Refresh Icon Cache (optional but helpful)
# This part is tricky, usually requires restarting explorer or using a tool.
# We'll try to notify the system of the change.
Write-Host "All shortcuts updated. You may need to restart Explorer to see changes."
```

```
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
import os

def create_japan26_icon(name, bg_color, fg_color, draw_func):
    size = 256
    img = Image.new('RGBA', (size, size), (0, 0, 0, 0))
    draw = ImageDraw.Draw(img)

    # Draw background circle
    margin = 20
    draw.ellipse([margin, margin, size - margin, size - margin], fill=bg_color)

    # Draw foreground symbol
    draw_func(draw, size, fg_color)

    # Save as ICO
    output_path = f'C:/Users/kyleh/.gemini/tmp/icons/japan26_{name}.ico'
    img.save(output_path, format='ICO', sizes=[(256, 256), (128, 128), (64, 64), (48, 48), (32, 32), (16, 16)])
    return output_path

def draw_firefox(draw, size, color):
    # Simplified fox/swirl
    center = size // 2
    r = 60
    draw.arc([center - r, center - r, center + r, center + r], 0, 270, fill=color, width=15)
    draw.ellipse([center - 20, center - 20, center + 20, center + 20], fill=color)

# Japan 26 Palette
japan_bg = "#E7E7CF"
japan_accent = "#959581"

os.makedirs('C:/Users/kyleh/.gemini/tmp/icons', exist_ok=True)

path = create_japan26_icon('firefox', japan_bg, japan_accent, draw_firefox)
print(f"Created icon at {path}")
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
import os

def create_japan26_bold_icon(name, color, draw_func):
    size = 256
    img = Image.new('RGBA', (size, size), (0, 0, 0, 0))
    draw = ImageDraw.Draw(img)

    # Draw foreground symbol
    draw_func(draw, size, color)

    # Save as ICO
    output_path = f'C:/Users/kyleh/.gemini/tmp/icons/japan26_{name}_bold.ico'
    img.save(output_path, format='ICO', sizes=[(256, 256), (128, 128), (64, 64), (48, 48), (32, 32), (16, 16)])
    return output_path

def draw_firefox_bold(draw, size, color):
    # Large thick circle/swirl
    c = size // 2
    draw.ellipse([20, 20, size-20, size-20], outline=color, width=40)
    draw.pieslice([40, 40, size-40, size-40], 0, 270, fill=color)

# Japan 26 Accent
japan_accent = "#959581"

path = create_japan26_bold_icon('firefox', japan_accent, draw_firefox_bold)
```
